BIQAI-FULL: The assurance is part of their supplication, praising God and asking Him to fulfill the Prophet’s promise; they fear being charged for unchosen inner whisperings they cannot prevent. DURR-FULL reporting أبو هريرة: The Prophet said God pardons his community's inward whisperings so long as they do not act on or speak them. [7 notes: TAB-FULL(ابن عباس); THALABI(بعضهم); BAGHAWI-FULL; IBNJAWZI-ZAD(قوم); BIQAI-FULL; DURR-FULL(أبو هريرة)]
- 2:286/q21/p5 The burden is passionate love. — THALABI reporting محمد بن عبد الوهاب: Muhammad ibn Abd al-Wahhab interprets the unbearable burden as passionate love. BAGHAWI-FULL reporting محمد بن عبد الوهاب: Muhammad ibn ʿAbd al-Wahhab identifies it as passionate love. IBNJAWZI-ZAD reporting الثوري عن منصور عن إبراهيم: A second view takes the unbearable thing to be love. ABUHAYYAN-FULL reporting النخعي: Al-Nakhaʿi interprets the unbearable burden as love. ABUHAYYAN-FULL reporting محمد بن عبد الوهاب: Muhammad ibn Abd al-Wahhab glosses the burden as passionate infatuation. THALABI reporting يحيى: Yahya says he would not punish lovers because their sins arise from compulsion rather than choice. BAGHAWI-FULL reporting إبراهيم: Ibrahim says the burden is love. ITQAN reporting أبو هريرة: Al-Daylamī transmits a Prophetic report from Abū Hurayra that the last two verses of al-Baqara are Qur'an, healing, and beloved to God. Against: ITQAN reporting من قال: A rejected interpretation glosses the prayer not to be burdened beyond capacity as referring to love and passion; al-Kawashi records it. [8 notes: THALABI(محمد بن عبد الوهاب,يحيى); BAGHAWI-FULL(إبراهيم,محمد بن عبد الوهاب); IBNJAWZI-ZAD(الثوري عن منصور عن إبراهيم); ABUHAYYAN-FULL(النخعي,محمد بن عبد الوهاب); ITQAN(أبو هريرة); ITQAN(من قال)-]
- 2:286/q21/p6 The burden is enemies' gloating. — THALABI reporting وهب بن منبه: Wahb ibn Munabbih reports that Job said the hardest part of his long affliction was his enemies’ gloating. BAGHAWI-FULL: An unnamed explanation identifies the burden as the gloating of enemies. ABUHAYYAN-FULL reporting قيل: Another explanation is the gloating of enemies. ABUHAYYAN-FULL reporting وهب: Wahb reports that Job was asked what was hardest in his affliction and answered: the enemies’ gloating. [4 notes: THALABI(وهب بن منبه); BAGHAWI-FULL; ABUHAYYAN-FULL(قيل,وهب)]
- 2:286/q21/p7 The burden is separation or estrangement. — BAGHAWI-FULL: Another unnamed explanation identifies it as separation and estrangement. [1 notes: BAGHAWI-FULL]

## 2:286/q22 What does طَاقَةَ mean and how is it formed?  [طَاقَةَ · meaning]
- 2:286/q22/p1 Ṭāqa is ability, an irregular shortened verbal noun of iṭāqa. — ZAJJAJ: Mā lā ṭāqata lanā bihi means a burden that weighs heavily on us. WAHIDI-BASIT: Ṭāqa is a verbal noun from iṭāqa, like ṭāʿa from iṭāʿa, and can stand in place of the verbal noun. RAZI-FULL: Ṭāqa is a verbal noun from iṭāqa, like ṭāʿa from iṭāʿa and ijāba from ijāba, and is used in place of the verbal noun. RAZI-FULL reporting أصحابنا: Al-Razi’s school rejects the hardship-only gloss: it would duplicate the preceding prayer, and ṭāqa means ability, so the apparent sense is what one has no power to do; “hard” is only figurative. ABUHAYYAN-FULL: Ṭāqa is an anomalous verbal noun, not a productive pattern; it can mean absolute inability or a burden that is possible but severely difficult, mapping respectively to punishment or duties. SAMIN-DURR: Ṭāqa means ability and is an irregularly shortened verbal noun of aṭāqa; the expected form is iṭāqa, like other exceptional maṣdars such as ghāra, jāba, and nabāt, and it is not productive. IBNASHUR-FULL: Ṭāqa derives from iṭāqa, with the hamza dropped for ease, as in jāba/ijāba and ṭāʿa/iṭāʿa. SAMIN-DURR: The author supports the exceptional verbal-noun pattern with Qurʾān 71:17 and a poetic example of ʿaṭāʾ. [8 notes: ZAJJAJ; WAHIDI-BASIT; RAZI-FULL(أصحابنا); ABUHAYYAN-FULL; SAMIN-DURR; IBNASHUR-FULL]

## 2:286/q23 How are the two burden petitions related?  [وَلَا تَحْمِلْ وَلَا تُحَمِّلْنَا · rhetoric]
- 2:286/q23/p1 The second request repeats the first in meaning. — KASHSHAF-FULL: The second request repeats the first in meaning. [1 notes: KASHSHAF-FULL]

## 2:286/q24 How do the forms of the burden verbs work?  [وَلَا تَحْمِلْ وَلَا تُحَمِّلْنَا · grammar]
turns on: The alternatives differ over the wording, scope, or evidence cited in the notes.
- 2:286/q24/p1 The supplicatory verbs are jussive because they express requests; lā tuḥammil is phrased as a prohibition. — NAHHAS: The supplicatory verbs are jussive because they express requests; lā tuḥammil is phrased as a prohibition. [1 notes: NAHHAS]
- 2:286/q24/p2 Ibn Abi ʿAblah reads the word with fatḥa on the wāw. — KASHSHAF-FULL reporting ابن أبي عبلة: Ibn Abi ʿAblah reads the word with fatḥa on the wāw. [1 notes: KASHSHAF-FULL(ابن أبي عبلة)]
- 2:286/q24/p3 A reading doubles the tāʾ in tuḥammil to add emphasis. — BAYDAWI-FULL: A reading doubles the tāʾ in tuḥammil to add emphasis. [1 notes: BAYDAWI-FULL]
- 2:286/q24/p4 Abu reads the verb in an intensive form, comparable to the difference between جرح and جرّح. — ABUHAYYAN-FULL: Abu reads the verb in an intensive form, comparable to the difference between جرح and جرّح. [1 notes: ABUHAYYAN-FULL]
- 2:286/q24/p5 Al-Zamakhsharī distinguishes the two doubled forms: the first intensifies carrying upon someone, while the second causatively shifts ḥamala from one object to two. — SAMIN-DURR reporting الزمخشري: Al-Zamakhsharī distinguishes the two doubled forms: the first intensifies carrying upon someone, while the second causatively shifts ḥamala from one object to two. [1 notes: SAMIN-DURR(الزمخشري)]
- 2:286/q24/p6 Ubayy's doubled mīm in lā tuḥammil ʿalaynā intensifies the action. — SAMIN-DURR reporting أبي: Ubayy's doubled mīm in lā tuḥammil ʿalaynā intensifies the action. [1 notes: SAMIN-DURR(أبي)]
- 2:286/q24/p7 Aṣāran is also read in the plural, and one reading doubles the tāʾ in tuḥammil. — ALUSI-FULL: Aṣāran is also read in the plural, and one reading doubles the tāʾ in tuḥammil. [1 notes: ALUSI-FULL]
- 2:286/q24/p8 The shadda in this phrase intensifies the act of imposing a burden; the other form changes the verb from one object to two. — KASHSHAF-FULL: The shadda in this phrase intensifies the act of imposing a burden; the other form changes the verb from one object to two. [1 notes: KASHSHAF-FULL]

## 2:286/q25 What does وَٱعْفُ عَنَّا request?  [وَٱعْفُ عَنَّا · interpretation]
- 2:286/q25/p1 Pardon removes sin's punishment or traces. — TAB-FULL: The threat in 2:284 does not target an unacted sinful inclination, since God promised pardon for minor sins when major sins are avoided; it targets concealed unbelief. TAB-FULL: Ṭabarī says the believers ask pardon for falling short in commanded duties, so God will overlook the shortfall and not punish them, even when the duties are light. TAB-FULL reporting ابن زيد: Ibn Zayd says pardon is sought for falling short in any of God’s commands. IBNABIHATIM reporting أبو هريرة، محمد بن كعب، سعيد بن جبير: Abū Hurayra, Muḥammad b. Kaʿb, and Saʿīd b. Jubayr are also reported to give this answer of pardon. THALABI: The plea واعف عنا asks God to overlook and pardon human shortcomings and sins. THALABI reporting بعضهم: Another interpretation assigns pardon to minor sins, forgiveness to grave sins, and mercy to the weighing of deeds despite spiritual bankruptcy. THALABI reporting بعضهم: A further interpretation asks pardon during death's agony, forgiveness in the grave's darkness, and mercy there as well. QUSHAYRI: “Pardon us” concerns the present, while “forgive us” concerns the future. WAHIDI-ASBAB reporting النبي: The Prophet says God has pardoned his community for thoughts they do not act on or speak aloud. JISHUMI: Another interpretation distinguishes pardon for minor sins from forgiveness of major ones. IBNATIYYA-FULL: Pardon concerns what believers committed and disclosed; forgiveness asks God to conceal what He knows; mercy asks for freely initiated favor. RAZI-FULL: Al-Razi’s alternative is that God takes the sinner through punishment while the sinner clings to God for pardon, so each is figuratively taking the other. RAZI-FULL: ʿAfw means removing punishment, whereas maghfira conceals the sin and protects the sinner from the shame and exposure of punishment in the grave. QURTUBI-FULL: ʿAfw means leaving a sin without punishing its doer. BAYDAWI-FULL: “Pardon us” means erase our sins. NASAFI-FULL: The requests are not redundant: pardon erases major sins, while forgiveness covers minor sins. ABUHAYYAN-FULL: غفرانك asks pardon for falling short, or recognizes that even perfect worship falls short beside divine majesty. ABUHAYYAN-FULL reporting قيل: A further explanation says God takes the sinner in punishment while the sinner appeals to God for pardon and generosity, so each is figuratively described as taking the other. ABUHAYYAN-FULL reporting قيل: Another view makes the prayer hypothetical: even if forgetfulness could warrant accountability, pardon is requested because constant vigilance would be burdensome. ABUHAYYAN-FULL: Pardon removes punishment; forgiveness conceals the sin and protects from humiliation; mercy bestows beneficence, with each petition increasing in scope and intensity. ABUHAYYAN-FULL reporting الراغب: Al-Raghib similarly defines pardon as removing sin’s punishment, forgiveness as covering it and replacing it with kindness, and mercy as an outpouring of beneficence. IBNKATHIR-FULL: Pardon concerns shortcomings between the servant and God; forgiveness concerns failings toward other people, whom God is asked not to expose to the servant’s ugly deeds; mercy asks protection from future sin. IBNKATHIR-FULL: A sinner needs God to pardon him, conceal his faults from people, and preserve him from falling into the same kind of sin again. BIQAI-FULL: They ask God to remove the punishment for all sins, then not to mention those sins at all; pardon addresses bodily punishment and forgiveness spiritual torment. BIQAI-FULL reporting الحرالي: Al-Ḥarālī says mercy raises the pardoned and forgiven sinner to the same share of mercy as one who never sinned. BIQAI-FULL: After asking for pardon, forgiveness, and mercy, believers seek the fruit of divine guardianship: victory through speech and arms. BIQAI-FULL reporting أبو الحسن الحرالي: Al-Harali says the surah's forgiveness treats sin as though it never occurred, and mercy raises the pardoned sinner to high ranks; its opening is guidance and its close is vicegerency. DURR-FULL reporting ابن زيد: Ibn Zayd distinguishes pardon for falling short in commands, forgiveness for violating prohibitions, and mercy as necessary to fulfill commands and avoid prohibitions; no one is saved except through mercy. BURSEVI: Pardon means removing the traces of sins; forgiveness means concealing faults so the believer is not exposed before people. BURSEVI: Pardon and forgiveness precede the request for mercy because clearing away faults comes before adorning the servant with favor. ALUSI-FULL: To pardon is to erase the traces of sins by withholding punishment. ALUSI-FULL reporting قيل: An alternative assigns pardon to deeds, forgiveness to words, and mercy to the weighing of the scales. IBNASHUR-FULL: Alternatively, pardon, forgiveness, and mercy generalize the preceding specific requests and are their foundations, making this one encompassing prayer. TABATABAI: The three pleas progress from specific effect to general source: pardon removes sin's effect or recorded punishment, forgiveness removes and veils its inward state, and mercy is God's gift covering both. [34 notes: TAB-FULL(ابن زيد); IBNABIHATIM(أبو هريرة، محمد بن كعب، سعيد بن جبير); THALABI(بعضهم); QUSHAYRI; WAHIDI-ASBAB(النبي); JISHUMI; IBNATIYYA-FULL; RAZI-FULL; QURTUBI-FULL; BAYDAWI-FULL; NASAFI-FULL; ABUHAYYAN-FULL(الراغب,قيل); IBNKATHIR-FULL; BIQAI-FULL(أبو الحسن الحرالي,الحرالي); DURR-FULL(ابن زيد); BURSEVI; ALUSI-FULL(قيل); IBNASHUR-FULL; TABATABAI]

## 2:286/q26 What does وَٱغْفِرْ لَنَا request?  [وَٱغْفِرْ لَنَا · interpretation]
- 2:286/q26/p1 Forgiveness conceals faults and averts exposure or punishment. — TAB-FULL: Ṭabarī explains forgiveness as concealing a slip between the servant and God instead of exposing and disgracing the sinner. TAB-FULL reporting ابن زيد: Ibn Zayd also applies the request for forgiveness to committing something God prohibited. IBNABIHATIM reporting أبو هريرة، سعيد بن جبير، السدي، مقاتل بن حيان: Abū Hurayra, Saʿīd b. Jubayr, al-Suddī, and Muqātil b. Ḥayyān are also reported to give this answer of forgiveness. NAHHAS: The same grammatical analysis applies to “forgive us,” “have mercy on us,” and “give us victory.” THALABI reporting الآخرون: Others take it as unintentional ignorance or oversight, which al-Tha'labi calls more correct because deliberate sin is not automatically forgiven, though it remains subject to God’s will unless it is unbelief. THALABI: Forgiveness asks God to cover sins and forgo punishment; mercy is needed because people cannot obey or avoid sin without divine aid. RAZI-FULL: Supplication can express humility rather than ask for an uncertain outcome; al-Razi compares requests for just judgment, fulfillment of promises, and forgiveness for repentant believers. QURTUBI-FULL: Forgiveness means covering sins; ghafr is concealment. BAYDAWI-FULL: “Forgive us” means conceal our faults and do not expose us through punishment. ABUHAYYAN-FULL reporting قيل: Another rationale maps faith to human origin, hearing and obeying to present life, and the plea for forgiveness onward to the afterlife. ABUHAYYAN-FULL reporting سيبويه: Sibawayh parses غفرانك as a verbal noun governed by an implicit command, “forgive us with Your forgiveness”; this makes the phrase a request. ABUHAYYAN-FULL: Other grammarians parse غفرانك as an object of asking or as a nominative subject meaning “Your forgiveness is our aim.” ALUSI-FULL: To forgive is to conceal what is ugly and disclose what is beautiful. ELMALILI: He explains forgiveness as covering and concealing people’s faults, suppressing their blemishes, and not exposing them publicly. KURANYOLU-TEFSIR: The closing supplication teaches that sincere servants have inadvertent lapses which the Lord may forgive, and that Islam’s final, complete religion is founded on ease rather than burdensome worship. STUDYQURAN: Thy forgiveness is an entreaty with an implied imperative: Grant us Thy forgiveness. [16 notes: TAB-FULL(ابن زيد); IBNABIHATIM(أبو هريرة، سعيد بن جبير، السدي، مقاتل بن حيان); NAHHAS; THALABI; RAZI-FULL; QURTUBI-FULL; BAYDAWI-FULL; ABUHAYYAN-FULL(سيبويه,قيل); ALUSI-FULL; ELMALILI; KURANYOLU-TEFSIR; STUDYQURAN; THALABI(الآخرون)+]

## 2:286/q27 What does وَٱرْحَمْنَا request?  [وَٱرْحَمْنَآ · interpretation]
- 2:286/q27/p1 Mercy grants divine favor, aid, or salvation. — TAB-FULL: Ṭabarī says only God’s mercy saves people from punishment; deeds cannot save without mercy, so the prayer also asks for guidance to what pleases God. QUSHAYRI: They ask for mercy and victory in every state because they have no protector besides God; victory also means overcoming whatever distracts them from Him. JISHUMI: God’s mercy is provision in this world and Paradise in the next. KASHSHAF-FULL: This declares God’s justice and mercy; people could physically pray more than five times, fast longer than a month, or perform pilgrimage more than once. RAZI-FULL: After seeking release from bodily punishment and spiritual disgrace, believers seek reward: mercy asks for bodily delights of Paradise, while “You are our Protector” asks for spiritual reward culminating in witnessing divine majesty. QURTUBI-FULL: Mercy here is a favor that God initiates for people. BAYDAWI-FULL: “Have mercy on us” means show tenderness and favor. IBNKATHIR-FULL: God never imposes on a person more than they can bear; this expresses His kindness, mercy, and beneficence toward creation. BIQAI-FULL: The shift from direct address to speaking of God in the third person is a deferential appeal to His majesty and to His patience, mercy, and compassion. BIQAI-FULL: Repeating “our Lord” at the start of each request recalls the greatness of the occasion and God’s nurturing, kindness, and mercy. ALUSI-FULL: This is a fresh divine statement after believers accept the commands, revealing the grace and mercy built into the obligations before they ask for it. ALUSI-FULL: This easing is grace, mercy, or special favor upon this community. ALUSI-FULL: Mercy is gracious concern for the servants that brings increase. ELMALILI: Every community’s law is a divinely imposed burden: believers receive a straight law as mercy, while unbelievers receive a crooked one as the effect of justice and wrath; what counts as justice differs with their spiritual condition, and God turns hearts, so the plea asks for steadfastness in Islam. ELMALILI: The request for mercy asks God to bestow blessings on the petitioners. TABATABAI: Repeating “our Lord” four times evokes divine mercy and recalls the speakers' servitude and abasement. [16 notes: TAB-FULL; QUSHAYRI; JISHUMI; KASHSHAF-FULL; RAZI-FULL; QURTUBI-FULL; BAYDAWI-FULL; IBNKATHIR-FULL; BIQAI-FULL; ALUSI-FULL; ELMALILI; TABATABAI]

[1 notes: IBNASHUR-FULL]
- 2:286/q28/p23 The three pleas progress from specific effect to general source: pardon removes sin's effect or recorded punishment, forgiveness removes and veils its inward state, and mercy is God's gift covering both. — TABATABAI: The three pleas progress from specific effect to general source: pardon removes sin's effect or recorded punishment, forgiveness removes and veils its inward state, and mercy is God's gift covering both. [1 notes: TABATABAI]
- 2:286/q28/p24 Pardon, forgiveness, and mercy seek relief from sins. — MUQATIL: Pardon, forgiveness, and mercy seek relief from sins. [1 notes: MUQATIL]

## 2:286/q29 What does مَوْلَىٰنَا mean?  [مَوْلَىٰنَا · meaning]
turns on: The alternatives differ over the wording, scope, or evidence cited in the notes.
- 2:286/q29/p1 Mawlā means guardian, protector, helper, or manager of affairs. — IBNQUTAYBA-GHARIB: Mawlānā means our guardian or protector. WAHIDI-BASIT reporting ابن عباس: Ibn Abbas and the exegetes explain Mawlana as our helper or protector, as in the verses calling God the believers' wali or mawla. WAHIDI-BASIT: Anyone joined to you and protected by your strength is your mawla; this explains calling paternal kin mawali, while poetry distinguishes allied clients from relatives and mawla can mean a guardian of one's affairs. JISHUMI: Mawlā here means our guardian and protector, the one with greatest claim over us. BAGHAWI-FULL: God is their helper, protector, and patron. IBNATIYYA-FULL: Calling God our mawla praises Him, draws the supplicant near, and thanks Him; mawla derives from wali and denotes guardianship. TABRISI: Mawlānā means our protector, the one entitled to direct our affairs, and our helper. IBNJAWZI-ZAD: Anta mawlānā means “You are our protector.” RAZI-FULL: Here Mawla means helper or protector, as in 8:40, 2:257, 66:4, and 47:11. QURTUBI-FULL: Mawlana means our protector and guardian. QURTUBI-FULL: Mawlana means our protector and helper. SAMIN-DURR: The fāʾ marks causation: because God is their protector and manages their affairs, they ask Him to grant victory over their enemies. IBNKATHIR-FULL: Mawlana means our protector and helper; believers trust and rely on God, with no power or strength except through Him. BIQAI-FULL: Biqāʿī explains mawlā as the enduring guardian who maintains care for those who entrust Him with what they cannot accomplish themselves. BIQAI-FULL: Mawlā also means the one who brings guardianship to fruition through closeness and turning toward the ward; the petition seeks bodily reward in paradise and spiritual delight in vision. BURSEVI: The plea asks God to help against disbelievers and repel their harm, as a guardian should defend those in his care; victory may come through conquest, argument, or defense. ALUSI-FULL: When mawlā and sayyid are both used, Arabic usage puts mawlā first; al-Khansāʾ's verse is cited as evidence, though Al-Alusi has reservations about the criticism of the reverse order. ALUSI-FULL: The clause may be understood as an instruction to say: You are our patron. ALUSI-FULL: The fāʾ signals causation: because God is their patron and manager, they ask Him to grant victory, like asking generosity from a generous person. IBNASHUR-FULL: The appeal to God's patronage rests on a patron's duty to treat dependents gently. IBNASHUR-FULL: The فاء derives the plea for victory from God's role as patron, since a patron is expected to aid those under his care. IBNASHUR-FULL: The request's derivation from God's established guardianship over believers strengthens its insistence and recalls 2:257. TABATABAI: Mawlā means a supporter who takes charge of the supported person's affairs, not just any helper; God is believers' guardian and protector. WAHIDI-BASIT reporting يونس: Yunus explains the Prophet's saying Whoever has me as his mawla, Ali is his mawla to mean whoever has me as his wali. WAHIDI-BASIT: The report calling Muzayna, Juhayna, Aslam, and Ghifar God's and His Messenger's mawali means they are His allies; Al-Ajjaj's verse likewise uses mawali for allies of truth. [25 notes: IBNQUTAYBA-GHARIB; WAHIDI-BASIT(ابن عباس,يونس); JISHUMI; BAGHAWI-FULL; IBNATIYYA-FULL; TABRISI; IBNJAWZI-ZAD; RAZI-FULL; QURTUBI-FULL; SAMIN-DURR; IBNKATHIR-FULL; BIQAI-FULL; BURSEVI; ALUSI-FULL; IBNASHUR-FULL; TABATABAI]
- 2:286/q29/p2 Mawlā means master or owner. — THALABI: Calling God our master means asking Him as helper, protector, guardian, and patron to grant victory over the disbelievers. MAWARDI-FULL: Mawlana means either our master and owner or our protector and helper. KASHSHAF-FULL: “Our mawlā” may mean our master, since we are His servants; our helper; or the one who manages our affairs. KASHSHAF-FULL: They ask for victory because a master should aid his servants, because this is God’s customary way, or because managing their affairs is His concern. RAZI-FULL: Calling God their Mawla expresses utmost humility and dependence: God gives every blessing and honor, as a guardian tends a child or a master orders a servant’s affairs. BAYDAWI-FULL: Mawlā here means our master. BAYDAWI-FULL: The plea follows from a master’s duty to aid his clients against enemies; alternatively, “the unbelieving people” means unbelievers generally. NASAFI-FULL: Mawlā can mean our master, helper, or the one who manages our affairs. NASAFI-FULL: The request for victory follows because a master is expected to aid his servants. ABUHAYYAN-FULL: Mawla derives from waliya and can function as a verbal, temporal, or locative noun; in the sense of ruler, master, helper, or kinsman it is a lexicalized noun. ABUHAYYAN-FULL: The ف in فانصرنا signals causation: because God is their master and manager, they ask Him to help them against enemies and instill courage while weakening the enemy. BURSEVI: Mawlānā can mean our master while we are His servants, our helper, or the one who manages our affairs. ALUSI-FULL: Mawlānā means our owner and master, or the one who manages our affairs; it is a verbal noun used for an agent. ELMALILI: Mawlana means our patron, owner and master, guardian of our affairs, and helper; Elmalılı links it to the declaration that God is the protector of those who believe. [14 notes: THALABI; MAWARDI-FULL; KASHSHAF-FULL; RAZI-FULL; BAYDAWI-FULL; NASAFI-FULL; ABUHAYYAN-FULL; BURSEVI; ALUSI-FULL; ELMALILI]

## 2:286/q30 How is مَوْلَىٰنَا formed?  [مَوْلَىٰنَا · grammar]
turns on: The alternatives differ over the wording, scope, or evidence cited in the notes.
- 2:286/q30/p1 Mawlā derives from waliya and denotes a guardian or agent. — TAB-FULL: Ṭabarī derives mawla as the form mafʿal from waliya; the yāʾ becomes alif because the preceding lām, the word’s second radical, is open. WAHIDI-BASIT: God is our wali by helping us and by directing our affairs; the verb is wali, yali, wilayah, making Him wali and mawla. [2 notes: TAB-FULL; WAHIDI-BASIT]
- 2:286/q30/p2 Calling God our mawla praises Him, draws the supplicant near, and thanks Him; mawla derives from wali and denotes guardianship. — IBNATIYYA-FULL: Calling God our mawla praises Him, draws the supplicant near, and thanks Him; mawla derives from wali and denotes guardianship. [1 notes: IBNATIYYA-FULL]
- 2:286/q30/p3 Mawla derives from waliya and can function as a verbal, temporal, or locative noun; in the sense of ruler, master, helper, or kinsman it is a lexicalized noun. — ABUHAYYAN-FULL: Mawla derives from waliya and can function as a verbal, temporal, or locative noun; in the sense of ruler, master, helper, or kinsman it is a lexicalized noun. [1 notes: ABUHAYYAN-FULL]
- 2:286/q30/p4 Mawlānā means our owner and master, or the one who manages our affairs; it is a verbal noun used for an agent. — ALUSI-FULL: Mawlānā means our owner and master, or the one who manages our affairs; it is a verbal noun used for an agent. [1 notes: ALUSI-FULL]
- 2:286/q30/p5 The فاء derives the plea for victory from God's role as patron, since a patron is expected to aid those under his care. — IBNASHUR-FULL: The فاء derives the plea for victory from God's role as patron, since a patron is expected to aid those under his care. [1 notes: IBNASHUR-FULL]
- 2:286/q30/p6 The request's derivation from God's established guardianship over believers strengthens its insistence and recalls 2:257. — IBNASHUR-FULL: The request's derivation from God's established guardianship over believers strengthens its insistence and recalls 2:257. [1 notes: IBNASHUR-FULL]

## 2:286/q31 Why does فَٱنصُرْنَا follow God's patronage?  [أَنتَ مَوْلَىٰنَا فَٱنصُرْنَا · rhetoric]
- 2:286/q31/p1 The fāʾ makes God's patronage the reason for asking victory. — IBNATIYYA-FULL: The repeated root with different verb forms beautifies the style, as in the contrast between fa-mahhil and amhil. IBNJAWZI-ZAD: Fa-nṣurnā means “help us.” SAMIN-DURR: The author illustrates this causal appeal with “You are generous, so grant me” and “You are a hero, so protect your sanctuary.” IBNASHUR-FULL: The clause أنت مولانا is set apart as the reason for the preceding requests and as a premise for the following one. [4 notes: IBNATIYYA-FULL; IBNJAWZI-ZAD; SAMIN-DURR; IBNASHUR-FULL]

## 2:286/q32 What victory and what opponents are requested?  [فَٱنصُرْنَا عَلَى ٱلْقَوْمِ ٱلْكَٰفِرِينَ · interpretation]
turns on: The alternatives differ over the wording, scope, or evidence cited in the notes.
- 2:286/q32/p1 The plea asks God's help against disbelievers. — TAB-FULL reporting معاذ: Muʿadh used to say آمين after finishing this surah at its closing request for victory over the disbelievers. ZAJJAJ: He compares the request with the hypothetical granting of silver-roofed houses to disbelievers. IBNABIHATIM reporting أبو هريرة، سعيد بن جبير، محمد بن كعب، الضحاك: Abū Hurayra, Saʿīd b. Jubayr, Muḥammad b. Kaʿb, and al-Ḍaḥḥāk are also reported to give the answer that victory was granted. THALABI reporting بعضهم: Another interpretation is that enemies gloat over the afflicted person. JISHUMI: One view asks for help against disbelievers through subjugation. JISHUMI: Their request for help against disbelievers likewise shows that they have agency. BAGHAWI-FULL: The final plea asks God to grant victory over the disbelieving people. IBNATIYYA-FULL: Victory over unbelievers enables the law to stand, the Muslim word to prevail, and the way to obedience to remain open. IBNKATHIR-FULL: The disbelievers reject God’s religion, oneness, and Messenger, worship others, and associate created beings with God; the plea asks victory and the final outcome over them in this world and the next. BIQAI-FULL: Biqāʿī says the ending implies that fighting the disbelievers is obligatory and that they are the most hostile enemies. BIQAI-FULL reporting أبو الحسن الحرالي: Al-Harali traces the surah's close from belief and wealth to support against unbelievers, linking its ending back to its beginning and viewing al-Baqara as encompassing the Qur'an's meanings. BIQAI-FULL: The surah details the decree through its opening groups of believers, unbelievers, and hypocrites, then describes their seals, illnesses, rulings, and eventual recompense. ELMALILI: Elmalılı says the forthcoming Sura of Al-i Imran begins as a response to this prayer for victory. ELMALILI: The assurance of ease is glad tidings for Muhammad’s community, whose faith, obedience, sincerity, sound will, and adherence to truth suit this gift; unbelievers do not escape duty and instead impose desire-driven, unjust demands on one another. ELMALILI: The believers ask for material and spiritual victory over unbelievers in defending the truth and raising God’s word. IBNASHUR-FULL: Victory gathers worldly and otherworldly good: it brings security and flourishing, makes the faith prevail, prevents persecution, and draws people into Islam. TABATABAI: The final request for victory over unbelievers hints that this prayer belongs to Islam’s early emergence. TABATABAI: The text moves from each person’s individual faith to the group’s rejection of division, collective burdens, and plea for victory; faith belongs to individuals, while these events concern communities. TABATABAI: The sūra opens with the pious believing the unseen, praying, giving, accepting revelation, and affirming the afterlife, then contrasts unbelievers and hypocrites before closing with obedient believers. SAMARRAI-ASRAR: Baqara similarly links Iblis being among the disbelievers with the sūra’s closing prayer for victory over the disbelieving people. [20 notes: TAB-FULL(معاذ); ZAJJAJ; IBNABIHATIM(أبو هريرة، سعيد بن جبير، محمد بن كعب، الضحاك); THALABI(بعضهم); JISHUMI; BAGHAWI-FULL; IBNATIYYA-FULL; IBNKATHIR-FULL; BIQAI-FULL(أبو الحسن الحرالي); ELMALILI; IBNASHUR-FULL; TABATABAI; SAMARRAI-ASRAR]
- 2:286/q32/p2 Victory includes prevailing through proof and argument. — ZAJJAJ: The prayer asks for victory in establishing proof, war, and other affairs, so Islam may prevail as promised. WAHIDI-BASIT: The requested victory includes establishing the proof, prevailing in war and other affairs, and making the religion manifest over every religion as promised. WAHIDI-WAJIZ: God is their helper and manager of affairs; they ask to prevail in arguments, war, and other affairs so their religion triumphs as promised. WAHIDI-WAJIZ: They ask for victory in argument, war, and other affairs, and for their religion to prevail as promised. JISHUMI: Another view understands the requested help as argument and victory. TABRISI: The final plea asks God to help believers overcome unbelievers by force and by the strength of their argument. RAZI-FULL: The first rational argument invokes God's eternal knowledge that one who dies in unbelief will never believe; that foreknown nonbelief is incompatible with belief, making the command appear to require a contradiction and likewise raising the problem of compulsion. RAZI-FULL: Al-Rāzī deems the rational proofs decisive: when they conflict with an apparent scriptural meaning, one must preserve the proofs and interpret the apparent text, or undermine rational grounds for monotheism, prophecy, and scripture. RAZI-FULL reporting أصحابنا: They answer that Qurʾanic usage associates bearing with obligation, as in the human bearing of the trust at 33:72; even without that idiom, the phrase covers both punishment and obligation, so narrowing it needs proof. RAZI-FULL: The requested victory includes military struggle, debate by proof, and Islam’s political ascendancy over other religions, as promised at 9:33. QURTUBI-FULL reporting المهدوي وغيره وابن عطية: Al-Mahdawi and others offer the sense that no one is charged with another’s sin; Ibn Atiyya accepts the principle but says this verse is not its proof. BIQAI-FULL: Mentioning the opposing people’s strength urges sincere reliance on God, while calling them kāfirūn identifies those concealing the clear proofs described at the sura’s opening. [12 notes: ZAJJAJ; WAHIDI-BASIT; WAHIDI-WAJIZ; JISHUMI; TABRISI; RAZI-FULL(أصحابنا); QURTUBI-FULL(المهدوي وغيره وابن عطية); BIQAI-FULL; RAZI-FULL+]
- 2:286/q32/p3 Victory includes military and worldly ascendancy. — RAZI-FULL reporting أصحابنا: The author’s school rejects moral annihilation: reward for good and liability for evil can coexist, and the arrival of one does not erase the other. RAZI-FULL reporting الجبائي: Al-Jubbaʾi reads the verse conditionally: reward remains only if not invalidated, and punishment only if not expiated by repentance, since pure everlasting reward and punishment cannot coexist. RAZI-FULL: Al-Razi imagines two stations: outward religious law asks for no severe duties; the beginning of unveiling asks not to demand praise, gratitude, or knowledge commensurate with divine majesty, which humans cannot attain. Law precedes inner reality, explaining the order. RAZI-FULL reporting المحققون: Some spiritual authorities interpret victory as God’s angelic, spiritual power overcoming bodily forces that urge the soul toward what is other than God. ALUSI-FULL: The requested victory is against enemy combatants or, more broadly, all unbelievers. ALUSI-FULL reporting الحسن: As transmitted by Abu Hayyan from al-Hasan, the phrase is an implied command to say this in prayer: God teaches servants how to ask so He may grant and reward them. IBNASHUR-FULL: Alternatively, the Prophet and believers may be speaking, explaining their assent as understanding that only thoughts with voluntary outward effects or settled conviction incur account; Ibn Ashur says the following clause makes this unlikely. TABATABAI: The clause connects backward by explaining that God asks no more than people can hear and obey. KURANYOLU-TEFSIR: The early emigrants left everything for Medina and the Helpers welcomed them; the ending tells these early Muslims their standing with God as a reward and offers later followers a lesson in religious life. MUSLIM reporting عبد الله بن عباس: Ibn Abbas says the warning about hidden thoughts deeply troubled the believers; the Prophet told them to hear, obey, and submit, and Allah placed faith in their hearts before revealing 2:286. [10 notes: RAZI-FULL(أصحابنا,الجبائي,المحققون); ALUSI-FULL(الحسن); IBNASHUR-FULL; TABATABAI; KURANYOLU-TEFSIR; MUSLIM(عبد الله بن عباس)]
- 2:286/q32/p4 The opponents include devils. — BURSEVI: The request for victory also seeks protection from devils, since they belong among the disbelieving enemies. [1 notes: BURSEVI]
- 2:286/q32/p5 God protects believers and grants victory over unbelievers. — MUQATIL: God protects believers and grants victory over unbelievers. [1 notes: MUQATIL]
- 2:286/q32/p6 The petition shows that public calling to religion was the believers' first concern after committing themselves to hear and obey. — TABATABAI: The petition shows that public calling to religion was the believers' first concern after committing themselves to hear and obey. [1 notes: TABATABAI]
- 2:286/q32/p7 نصر normally takes على, while نجّى takes من; the Qur’an uses these usual prepositions elsewhere. — SAMARRAI-ASRAR-MUH: نصر normally takes على, while نجّى takes من; the Qur’an uses these usual prepositions elsewhere. [1 notes: SAMARRAI-ASRAR-MUH]

## 2:286/q33 How are the petition verbs inflected?  [لَا تُؤَاخِذْنَآ وَٱعْفُ عَنَّا · grammar]
turns on: The alternatives differ over the wording, scope, or evidence cited in the notes.
- 2:286/q33/p1 The Basrans treat waʿfu as an imperative in form but indeclinable; the Kūfans make it jussive, as with the following requests. — NAHHAS reporting البصريون: The Basrans treat waʿfu as an imperative in form but indeclinable; the Kūfans make it jussive, as with the following requests. [1 notes: NAHHAS(البصريون)]
- 2:286/q33/p2 Some call prohibitive la a request particle so the label includes supplication as well as prohibition; here la is a prayer asking God not to hold the speakers accountable. — BURHAN reporting بعضهم: Some call prohibitive la a request particle so the label includes supplication as well as prohibition; here la is a prayer asking God not to hold the speakers accountable. [1 notes: BURHAN(بعضهم)]
- 2:286/q33/p3 Prohibitive lā is used with a present verb, makes it jussive, and places the action in the future; it may express either prohibition or supplication. — ITQAN: Prohibitive lā is used with a present verb, makes it jussive, and places the action in the future; it may express either prohibition or supplication. [1 notes: ITQAN]
- 2:286/q33/p4 Ibn Asfur gives inconsistent accounts, alternately saying the governing verb may be stated or must remain implicit. — ABUHAYYAN-FULL reporting ابن عصفور: Ibn Asfur gives inconsistent accounts, alternately saying the governing verb may be stated or must remain implicit. [1 notes: ABUHAYYAN-FULL(ابن عصفور)]
- 2:286/q33/p5 The coordinating and answer uses of lā do not occur in the Qur’an. — ITQAN: The coordinating and answer uses of lā do not occur in the Qur’an. [1 notes: ITQAN]

## 2:286/q34 Did 2:286 abrogate the accounting of 2:284?  [* · sciences]
turns on: The alternatives differ over the wording, scope, or evidence cited in the notes.
- 2:286/q34/p1 2:286 lifted or abrogated the earlier accounting for unacted thoughts. — TAB-FULL reporting بعضهم: Some interpreters say that the earlier ruling about inward thoughts was abrogated by 2:286; Abu Hurayra reports people’s distress and God affirming their concluding petitions. TAB-FULL reporting ابن عباس: Ibn ʿAbbas explains that Ibn ʿUmar feared people would perish under 2:284; 2:286 then abrogated accountability for inner whisperings while retaining accountability for speech and deeds. TAB-FULL reporting ابن عباس: Ibn Abbas said Ibn Umar wept over 2:284 as the Companions had, and that 2:286 abrogated it. TAB-FULL reporting سعيد بن جبير: Saʿid ibn Jubayr reported that 2:286 abrogated the earlier statement that God would account for what people disclosed or concealed. TAB-FULL reporting عامر: Amir said the verse about hidden thoughts being accounted for was abrogated by the following verse, 2:286. TAB-FULL reporting الشعبي: Al-Shaʿbi said 2:286 abrogated 2:284, which had said that God accounts for what people disclose or hide. TAB-FULL reporting ابن عباس: Ibn ʿAbbas says 2:284 greatly distressed the Companions, who were told to say “we hear and obey”; it was abrogated through 2:285–286, excusing inner thoughts while holding them to actions. TAB-FULL reporting الشعبي: Al-Shaʿbi reported that the burden of 2:284 lasted until 2:286 came, whereupon the latter abrogated what preceded it. TAB-FULL reporting ابن مسعود: Ibn Masʿud said accountability for hidden thoughts preceded 2:286, which abrogated the earlier verse. TAB-FULL reporting مجاهد: Mujahid and his co-transmitters reported that 2:286 abrogated the earlier verse about disclosed or concealed thoughts. TAB-FULL reporting عكرمة، وعامر: Ikrima and Amir reported the same position: 2:286 abrogated the earlier accounting for hidden thoughts. TAB-FULL reporting الحسن: Al-Hasan said 2:286 erased 2:284, including its statement about what is disclosed or hidden. TAB-FULL reporting عائشة: Aisha said that 2:286 abrogated the earlier verse about disclosed or concealed thoughts. TAB-FULL reporting قتادة: Qatada reported that 2:286 abrogated the preceding verse about God accounting for disclosed or hidden thoughts. TAB-FULL reporting عبد الله بن مسعود: Abu Ubayda reports from Ibn Masʿud that the following verse, 2:286, abrogated the prior statement about hidden thoughts. TAB-FULL reporting السدي: Al-Suddi reports that people were initially charged for inner whisperings as well as deeds; their complaint led God to abrogate that rule because thoughts exceeded their capacity. IBNABIHATIM reporting أبو عبيدة بن عبدالله: Abū ʿUbayda b. ʿAbd Allāh says 2:284 was abrogated by the following verse, which assigns each soul its earned good and bad deeds. MAWARDI-FULL reporting أبو هريرة: Abu Hurayra reports that the Companions feared accountability for inner thoughts, after which the capacity verse was revealed; Ibn Masud also held this abrogation view. WAHIDI-ASBAB: Al-Wahidi states that the verse of capacity abrogated the earlier ruling about what people disclosed inwardly. WAHIDI-BASIT reporting ابن عباس وأكثر المفسرين: Ibn Abbas and most exegetes say this verse abrogated the burden of involuntary thoughts and whispers imposed by the earlier verse. WAHIDI-WAJIZ: This verse abrogates the believers' complaint about being held to account for intrusive thoughts and inner speech. IBNKATHIR-FULL: Ibn Kathir calls this the abrogating verse that lifts the Companions’ fear about 2:284’s accountability for hidden thoughts. DURR-FULL reporting ابن عباس: Salim reports that Ibn ʿUmar wept over 2:284; Ibn ʿAbbas says the Companions reacted similarly and that the following verse abrogated it. DURR-FULL reporting علي: ʿAli says the Companions were distressed that thoughts might be judged; this verse then abrogated the earlier accounting for thoughts. DURR-FULL reporting ابن مسعود: Ibn Masʿud says accounting preceded the revelation of “each soul has what it earned,” which abrogated the earlier verse. ITQAN: The warning that God will call people to account for what they conceal or reveal inwardly is said to be abrogated by the next verse’s statement that God burdens no soul beyond its capacity. SUYUTI-LUBAB reporting أبو هريرة: In Abu Hurayra’s account, God abrogated the earlier burden by revealing “God does not burden a soul beyond its capacity.” MUSLIM reporting أبو هريرة: The report explicitly says Allah abrogated the earlier passage about accounting for inner thoughts and then revealed the assurance that He burdens no soul beyond its capacity. [28 notes: TAB-FULL(ابن عباس,ابن مسعود,الحسن,السدي,الشعبي,بعضهم,سعيد بن جبير,عائشة,عامر,عبد الله بن مسعود,عكرمة، وعامر,قتادة,مجاهد); IBNABIHATIM(أبو عبيدة بن عبدالله); MAWARDI-FULL(أبو هريرة); WAHIDI-ASBAB; WAHIDI-BASIT(ابن عباس وأكثر المفسرين); WAHIDI-WAJIZ; IBNKATHIR-FULL; DURR-FULL(ابن عباس,ابن مسعود,علي); ITQAN; SUYUTI-LUBAB(أبو هريرة); MUSLIM(أبو هريرة)]
- 2:286/q34/p2 2:284 remains operative or is clarified and specified. — TAB-FULL: Al-Tabari prefers that 2:284 remains in force: abrogation requires a later ruling to negate the earlier one in every respect, and 2:286 does not negate divine accounting. ABUHAYYAN-FULL: The sound view is that the verse remains operative, with the later verse specifying uncontrollable whispers if those are included; settled intentions are reckoned with, believers forgiven and disbelievers or hypocrites punished. IBNASHUR-FULL: Calling this abrogation follows early scholars' terminology; Ibn Ashur says it is clarification and specification, since adjacent verses may have been revealed at different times and believers could have feared hardship in the interval. Against: TABATABAI reporting ما ذكروه: He rejects the view that these verses respond to 2:284’s accounting for hidden thoughts, record acceptance of an impossible duty, and then abrogate it; he prefers the sūra-wide reading. [3 notes: IBNASHUR-FULL; ABUHAYYAN-FULL+; TAB-FULL+; TABATABAI(ما ذكروه)-]

## 2:286/q35 What occasion and sequence of revelation are reported?  [* · reports]
- 2:286/q35/p1 The believers feared 2:284, affirmed obedience, and received the closing assurance and petitions. — MUSNAD-AHMAD reporting أبو هريرة: When 2:284 alarmed the Companions as beyond their capacity, the Prophet instructed them to say ‘We hear and obey’; after they accepted, God revealed 2:285–286 and replaced the burden with the limit of each soul's capacity. TAB-FULL reporting ابن عباس: Ibn ʿAbbas says Muslims shared Ibn ʿUmar’s distress over 2:284; 2:286 came afterward because inward whisperings exceeded their capacity, leaving the soul accountable for what it acquired in words and deeds. TAB-FULL reporting ابن زيد: Ibn Zayd reports that believers feared accountability for unacted thoughts; after they affirmed obedience rather than Israelite disobedience, revelation relieved them by limiting the matter to deeds, not thoughts in the heart. THALABI reporting ابن عمر: Ibn Umar weeps at the prospect of being taken to account for everything hidden; Ibn Abbas says Muslims shared this distress and that the capacity verse made fleeting thoughts no longer accountable unless expressed or acted on. THALABI reporting ابن عطاء والسدي وأكثر المفسرين: They say this answered believers’ fear that they could not repent of involuntary inner thoughts after 2:284; such thoughts exceeded their capacity. MAWARDI-FULL reporting ابن عباس: Ibn Abbas says the Companions were told to hear, obey, and submit after 2:284; when they did, faith entered their hearts and the final verses' petitions followed. WAHIDI-ASBAB reporting المفسرون: After a year under the burden, the exegetes say the verse of capacity brought relief and was revealed in response to the Companions' distress. KASHSHAF-FULL: Wusʿ is what a person can bear without strain or distress; God assigns only what fits capacity and is manageable, below the limit of exertion. IBNATIYYA-FULL: Believers feared the reckoning in 2:284 and seek protection from forgetfulness and error beyond human control; on this reading an impossible duty is possible. ABUHAYYAN-FULL: The wording could encompass passing thoughts, so the Companions were distressed until God clarified the intended scope and relieved them. ABUHAYYAN-FULL: These reports support asking relief from involuntary forgetfulness and error after the believers feared the prior verse’s reckoning. DURR-FULL reporting ابن عباس: Ibn ʿAbbas says the Companions shared Ibn ʿUmar’s distress; God then revealed 2:286, removing liability for inner whispering beyond their capacity and leaving each person accountable for acquired speech and deeds. SUYUTI-LUBAB reporting أبو هريرة: Abu Hurayra reports that the Companions feared the burden of 2:284; the Prophet taught them to say they heard and obeyed, after which 2:285 was revealed and 2:286 lifted the burden. Muslim also transmits a similar report from Ibn Abbas. BURSEVI: After 2:284 alarmed the Companions, they knelt before the Prophet, fearing they could not bear accounting for inner thoughts. He warned against answering like earlier scriptural communities; they affirmed obedience and sought forgiveness. After 2:285 was revealed, 2:286 eased the burden: the account concerns deliberate resolve to do evil, not unavoidable passing thoughts. IBNASHUR-FULL reporting أبو هريرة، ابن عباس: In a Muslim report from Abu Hurayra and Ibn Abbas, companions feared the accountability verse, the Prophet told them to hear, obey, and submit, God strengthened their faith, and then this verse was revealed to lift the burden. TAB-FULL reporting الزهري: Al-Zuhri recounts Ibn ʿUmar’s tears and fear of being judged for inner thoughts, then Ibn ʿAbbas’s report that 2:286 relieved Muslims of that burden. [16 notes: MUSNAD-AHMAD(أبو هريرة); TAB-FULL(ابن زيد,ابن عباس,الزهري); THALABI(ابن عطاء والسدي وأكثر المفسرين,ابن عمر); MAWARDI-FULL(ابن عباس); WAHIDI-ASBAB(المفسرون); KASHSHAF-FULL; IBNATIYYA-FULL; ABUHAYYAN-FULL; DURR-FULL(ابن عباس); SUYUTI-LUBAB(أبو هريرة); BURSEVI; IBNASHUR-FULL(أبو هريرة، ابن عباس); ABUHAYYAN-FULL+]

## 2:286/q36 What responses to the prayers are reported?  [* · reports]
turns on: The alternatives differ over the wording, scope, or evidence cited in the notes.
- 2:286/q36/p1 God answered each successive petition affirmatively. — MUSNAD-AHMAD reporting ابن عباس عن النبي: Ibn Abbas reports that 2:284 troubled believers; the Prophet told them to answer ‘We hear and obey,’ and God then revealed the closing passage of al-Baqara, including 2:286. MUSNAD-AHMAD reporting العلاء: Al-Ala transmits affirmative answers to the petitions against forgetfulness, burdens, and inability, then quotes the final request for pardon, mercy, and help. TAB-FULL reporting أبو هريرة: When 2:284 was revealed, people feared accountability for inner thoughts and asked the Prophet; 2:286 followed, and God answered “yes” to their petitions. TAB-FULL reporting ابن عباس: Ibn ʿAbbas reports that 2:284 distressed believers; the Prophet told them to hear, obey, and submit, God strengthened their faith, then revealed 2:285 and affirmed each plea in 2:286 as fulfilled. TAB-FULL reporting سعيد بن جبير: Saʿid reports that believers feared being charged for thoughts they never enacted; 2:286 answered them, God said “I have done,” and this community received the closing verses uniquely. TAB-FULL reporting السدي: Al-Suddi reports that Gabriel confirmed to Muhammad after each petition in 2:286 that God had granted it. TAB-FULL reporting ابن عباس: Ibn Abbas reports God answering each of the petitions in the final verses with قد فعلت, meaning it has been granted. TAB-FULL reporting أبو هريرة: Abu Hurayrah reports the Prophet saying that God answered the plea not to hold believers accountable for forgetfulness or error with نعم. TAB-FULL reporting سعيد بن جبير: Saʿid ibn Jubayr reports God affirming the supplications with قد فعلت. ZAJJAJ reporting النبي: A report says God answered each petition in this prayer with I have done so, meaning it was accepted. IBNABIHATIM reporting الحسن: After Abū Bakr al-Hudhali told him the report, al-Ḥasan affirmed it and cited this prayer as Qur'anic evidence. IBNABIHATIM reporting ابن عباس: Ibn ʿAbbās reports God's answer: I will not hold you accountable. IBNABIHATIM reporting أبو هريرة: Abū Hurayra transmits the affirmative answer نعم to this plea. IBNABIHATIM reporting سعيد بن جبير: Saʿīd b. Jubayr likewise gives God's answer as I will not hold you accountable. IBNABIHATIM reporting ابن عباس: Ibn ʿAbbās glosses the plea as God's answer that He will not impose the burden on them; Saʿīd b. Jubayr also reports this gloss. IBNABIHATIM reporting أبو هريرة: Abū Hurayra transmits the affirmative answer نعم to this plea. IBNABIHATIM reporting ابن عباس: Ibn ʿAbbās reports God's answer to the plea for forgiveness as: I have forgiven you. IBNABIHATIM reporting ابن عباس، الضحاك، محمد بن كعب، السدي: Ibn ʿAbbās, al-Ḍaḥḥāk, Muḥammad b. Kaʿb, and al-Suddī report God's answer as: I have done so. IBNABIHATIM reporting سعيد بن جبير: Saʿīd b. Jubayr gives God's answer as: I will not impose it on you. IBNABIHATIM reporting مقاتل بن حيان: Muqātil interprets the unbearable burden as punishment that could turn people into apes and swine and punish them as earlier peoples were; Gabriel says the plea was granted, as Ibn Jurayj also reports. IBNABIHATIM reporting ابن عباس: Ibn ʿAbbās reports God's answer to the plea for pardon as: I have pardoned you. IBNABIHATIM reporting أبو هريرة: Abū Hurayra transmits God's affirmative response نعم to the plea for mercy and acknowledgment of His guardianship. IBNABIHATIM reporting سعيد بن جبير، ابن عباس، عطاء الخراساني، مقاتل بن حيان، السدي: Saʿīd b. Jubayr says God's answer is that He has shown mercy; Ibn ʿAbbās, ʿAṭāʾ al-Khurāsānī, Muqātil, and al-Suddī are reported similarly. IBNABIHATIM reporting ابن عباس: Ibn ʿAbbās reports God's answer as: you have been given victory over the disbelievers. IBNHIBBAN reporting ابن عباس: Ibn Abbas says 2:284 unsettled the Companions; the Prophet told them to affirm hearing, obedience, and surrender, after which God put faith in their hearts and answered the pleas not to be taken for forgetfulness or to bear the burden imposed on earlier peoples. IBNHIBBAN reporting أبو هريرة: Abu Hurayra reports that 2:284 distressed the Companions; the Prophet told them to affirm hearing and obedience, then God revealed 2:285 and 2:286, answering ‘yes’ to each plea in 2:286. THALABI: The verse answers an implicit plea about what the believers have been required to bear: God requires of a soul only what it can manage. THALABI reporting ابن عباس: Ibn Abbas transmits God's answer to the closing petitions: He has forgiven, excused, shown mercy, and granted victory, and will not burden people beyond their capacity. THALABI: In answer to whether a just God could impose beyond capacity, one view says God could do so because creation and command belong to Him, but He has promised not to. THALABI: Another answer stresses God's declared vastness, mercy, and denial of injustice, even though injustice could never be attributed to Him. QUSHAYRI: In answer to their plea, God replaced earlier peoples' destruction with forgiveness, their transformation with good deeds, and stones with mercy. WAHIDI-ASBAB reporting مسلم: The report closes with God's answer, ‘I have done so,’ to the supplication, and al-Wahidi says Muslim transmitted it through Abu Bakr ibn Abi Shayba and Waki. WAHIDI-BASIT: The author answers that the same objection applies to foreknowledge: if God's prior knowledge says someone will die an unbeliever, that person cannot change it; al-Shafiʿi says the Qadari are defeated if they concede foreknowledge. WAHIDI-BASIT reporting ابن عباس: Ibn Abbas says God answers the prayer section by section: He will not hold them accountable or impose burdens, and has pardoned, forgiven, shown mercy, and granted victory. BAGHAWI-FULL reporting أبو هريرة: Abu Hurayra reports that when the verse about hidden thoughts was revealed, it distressed the Companions, who said they could bear prayer, fasting, jihad, and charity but not this. The Prophet told them to answer as believers: “We hear and obey; forgive us.” God then revealed the believers' affirmation and replaced the former ruling with the declaration that no soul is charged beyond its capacity, followed by their petitions for pardon and help; the Prophet answered “yes” to each. BAGHAWI-FULL reporting ابن عباس: A report from Ibn Abbas says the ruling specifically concerns believers: God has made their religious obligations easy and only requires what they can do, as affirmed by 2:185 and 22:78. BAGHAWI-FULL: They cannot attain good action except through obedience granted by God, nor leave disobedience except through His mercy. BAGHAWI-FULL reporting ابن عباس: Ibn ʿAbbas, in a report from Saʿid ibn Jubayr, says God answered the closing pleas with forgiveness, pardon, mercy, and victory. IBNATIYYA-FULL reporting جبريل: Gabriel teaches Muhammad the petitions one by one and answers that God has granted each through the end of the sura. TABRISI reporting النبي: A report from the Prophet says that at every section of this supplication God answers, I have done so and granted it. RAZI-FULL: If God speaks the clause, it answers the believers' plea for forgiveness: after their obedience and request for pardon over deliberate shortcomings, He reassures them about inadvertent lapses. Against: RAZI-FULL: A fourth answer says the clause is spoken by the believers and therefore does not prove God's rule; al-Rāzī calls this weak, since God recounts and praises their words, so they must be truthful. RAZI-FULL reporting أصحابنا: Al-Razi’s associates answer by asking why God gave Jews harsh natures and this community refined ones; the explanation merely shifts the question about divine choice. QURTUBI-FULL: The prayer for victory was answered. QURTUBI-FULL reporting أبو هريرة: The account says 2:285 then praised the obedient believers, and God revealed 2:286 with “yes” affirmations to each request through the end of the verse. QURTUBI-FULL reporting علماؤنا: Muslim transmits the answer to the prayer through Abu Hurayra; scholars say the report’s “I have done so” and “yes” show transmission by meaning. QURTUBI-FULL reporting جبريل: In the Miʿraj narrative, the Prophet asks pardon for forgetfulness or error; Gabriel says this has been granted and these are lifted from the community. BAYDAWI-FULL reporting روي: A report says that when the Prophet made these supplications, he was told “I have done so” after each one. ABUHAYYAN-FULL: The final three pleas answer the first three: pardon answers “do not hold us accountable,” forgiveness answers “do not lay a burden,” and mercy answers “do not impose what we cannot bear.” ABUHAYYAN-FULL reporting السدي: Al-Suddi says the believers feared accountability when the verse came, then Gabriel told the Prophet that God had granted their request. SAMIN-DURR: Lahā mā kasabat is an independent explanatory sentence: being answerable only for one's own deeds illustrates that God does not burden a soul beyond its capacity. IBNKATHIR-FULL: God teaches His servants to ask Him and has undertaken to answer; forgetting means omitting a duty or doing a forbidden act forgetfully, while error is missing the legally correct action through ignorance. IBNKATHIR-FULL reporting أبو هريرة: In a report cited from Sahih Muslim, the Prophet says God answered this plea, Yes. IBNKATHIR-FULL reporting ابن عباس: In the report from Ibn Abbas cited here, God answers, I have done so. IBNKATHIR-FULL: The earlier transmitted answer to these pleas is that God said Yes; another report has Him say, I have done so. IBNKATHIR-FULL reporting ابن عباس: The answer is again reported as God saying Yes, or as in Muslim’s report from Ibn Abbas, I have done so. IBNKATHIR-FULL reporting أبو هريرة: In a report from Sahih Muslim, the Prophet says God answered this plea, Yes. IBNKATHIR-FULL reporting ابن عباس: Ibn Abbas transmits the answer to this plea as God saying, I have done so. BIQAI-FULL reporting ابن عباس: In al-Baghawī’s alternate narration from Ibn ʿAbbās, God answered “I have done so” and continued answering yes after each supplication through the end of the sura. BIQAI-FULL: Alternatively, God may be answering their “we hear and obey” by assuring them that uncommitted thoughts will not be counted, resolving the hardship of 2:284 unlike Israel’s disobedience. BIQAI-FULL: Although God may impose whatever He wills and is not answerable for His acts, the believers ask Him not to place a heavy burden on them. BIQAI-FULL: Having affirmed that earlier burdens were just, believers acknowledge God’s sovereign right to impose even more, then ask Him not to load them beyond capacity; form II evokes the labor of such a burden. DURR-FULL reporting ابن عباس: Ibn ʿAbbas reports that 2:284 deeply distressed the Companions; the Prophet told them to hear and obey, then 2:285–286 came, and God affirmed each petition in 2:286 with “I have done so.” DURR-FULL reporting ابن عباس: When this verse descended, believers worried about resisting involuntary thoughts; Gabriel answered that they cannot prevent such whisperings. DURR-FULL reporting النبي صلى الله عليه وسلم: The Prophet said God pardons his community's errors, forgetfulness, and coercion; al-Hasan affirmed this is expressed in the Quranic plea. DURR-FULL reporting السدي: When the plea against being taken to task for forgetting or erring was revealed, Gabriel told Muhammad that God had granted it. DURR-FULL reporting الضحاك: Gabriel brought the concluding supplications with a company of angels, and after each phrase God answered, 'That is yours.' ALUSI-FULL reporting أبو حيان: Abu Hayyan explains the sequence: pardon answers the plea not to punish for error, forgiveness the plea not to impose burdens, and mercy the plea not to impose the unbearable; hence no repeated rabbana. ALUSI-FULL: The Jews' claim that God was poor for asking a loan in al-Baqara is answered by Al ʿImran's declaration that God heard those who called Him poor, strengthening the sūra link. ELMALILI reporting الله: Elmalılı says Muslim and Tirmidhi report that when the Prophet prayed with these petitions, God answered, “So I have done.” ELMALILI: God does not burden a soul beyond its capacity, even though He could; duties stay below capacity without strain or hardship, as affirmed by 2:185, because of grace and mercy. IBNASHUR-FULL reporting النبي: At Uhud, after Abu Sufyan boasted of al-Uzza, the Prophet instructed the Muslims to answer that God is their patron and their opponents have no patron. TABATABAI: God has also granted servants a reciprocal claim upon Him: forgiveness, which every person, including prophets and messengers, needs for their own happiness. TABATABAI: Answering God through hearing and obedience applies only to what a person can understand and perform; incomprehensible or physically impossible acts cannot be obligations. MUSLIM reporting الله تعالى: In Abu Hurayra’s account, Allah answers yes to the petitions in 2:286: pardon for forgetfulness and error, relief from burdens and incapacity, forgiveness, mercy, and aid. MUSLIM reporting الله تعالى: Allah answers “I have done so” to the believers’ prayers for protection from error and burdens, and for forgiveness, mercy, and His guardianship. STUDYQURAN reporting God: Reports say God answers the believers' successive petitions with I have done so or Yes. STUDYQURAN reporting Gabriel: In another report, Gabriel tells the Prophet that God has answered the successive petitions. TIRMIDHI reporting ابن عباس: Ibn ʿAbbās says the revelation in 2:284 distressed the believers; the Prophet told them to answer that they heard and obeyed, God placed faith in their hearts, and revelation then came down through 2:286. TIRMIDHI reporting الله تعالى: In Ibn ʿAbbās’s report, God answers the requests not to punish forgetfulness or error, impose the former peoples’ burden, or demand the unbearable, and to pardon, forgive, and show mercy, with “I have done so.” ABUHAYYAN-FULL reporting ابن عباس وابن جبير والضحاك وعطاء: A second report says Gabriel brought the two verses down to the Prophet in Medina. ALUSI-FULL reporting الضحاك: Abu Saʿid and al-Bayhaqi transmit from al-Dahhak that Gabriel brought the verse with angels and told the Prophet after each clause, I grant you that, until he finished. [81 notes: MUSNAD-AHMAD(ابن عباس عن النبي,العلاء); TAB-FULL(أبو هريرة,ابن عباس,السدي,سعيد بن جبير); ZAJJAJ(النبي); IBNABIHATIM(أبو هريرة,ابن عباس,ابن عباس، الضحاك، محمد بن كعب، السدي,الحسن,سعيد بن جبير,سعيد بن جبير، ابن عباس، عطاء الخراساني، مقاتل بن حيان، السدي,مقاتل بن حيان); IBNHIBBAN(أبو هريرة,ابن عباس); THALABI(ابن عباس); QUSHAYRI; WAHIDI-ASBAB(مسلم); WAHIDI-BASIT(ابن عباس); BAGHAWI-FULL(أبو هريرة,ابن عباس); IBNATIYYA-FULL(جبريل); TABRISI(النبي); RAZI-FULL(أصحابنا); QURTUBI-FULL(أبو هريرة,جبريل,علماؤنا); BAYDAWI-FULL(روي); ABUHAYYAN-FULL(ابن عباس وابن جبير والضحاك وعطاء,السدي); SAMIN-DURR; IBNKATHIR-FULL(أبو هريرة,ابن عباس); BIQAI-FULL(ابن عباس); DURR-FULL(ابن عباس,السدي,الضحاك,النبي صلى الله عليه وسلم); ALUSI-FULL(أبو حيان,الضحاك); ELMALILI(الله); IBNASHUR-FULL(النبي); TABATABAI; MUSLIM(الله تعالى); STUDYQURAN(Gabriel,God); TIRMIDHI(ابن عباس,الله تعالى); RAZI-FULL-]
- 2:286/q36/p2 The reported answers were granted specifically to the Prophet. — TAB-FULL reporting الضحاك: Al-Dahhak says Gabriel told the Prophet to ask these petitions; God granted them specifically to the Prophet. DURR-FULL reporting أبو ذر: Abu Dharr reports that these petitions were granted specifically to the Prophet. DURR-FULL reporting الضحاك: Al-Dahhak says the Prophet asked God for these petitions and received them specifically for himself. [3 notes: TAB-FULL(الضحاك); DURR-FULL(أبو ذر,الضحاك)]
- 2:286/q36/p3 Gabriel taught the prayer and God granted it. — MUQATIL: Gabriel taught the prayer and God granted it. [1 notes: MUQATIL]

## 2:286/q37 What are the origin and virtues of the closing verses?  [* · sciences]
turns on: The alternatives differ over the wording, scope, or evidence cited in the notes.
- 2:286/q37/p1 Reciting the closing verses at night suffices the reciter, possibly for night prayer. — ABDURRAZZAQ reporting علقمة بن قيس: ʿAlqama ibn Qays says reciting the closing verses of Sūrat al-Baqara at night suffices in place of that night’s prayer vigil. KASHSHAF-FULL reporting النبي صلى الله عليه وسلم: A report says two verses from Paradise’s treasures were written by the Merciful before creation two thousand years earlier; reciting them after the night prayer suffices instead of night vigil. KASHSHAF-FULL reporting النبي صلى الله عليه وسلم: The Prophet said that reciting al-Baqara’s final two verses at night suffices the reciter. IBNATIYYA-FULL reporting أبو مسعود عقبة بن عمرو: Abu Masud Uqba ibn Amr transmits that reciting the last two verses at night suffices in place of night prayer. TABRISI reporting النبي: A well-known Prophetic report says whoever recites the final two verses of al-Baqara at night is sufficed for that night's vigil. QURTUBI-FULL reporting أبو مسعود الأنصاري: Muslim transmits from Abu Masud al-Ansari that the Prophet said whoever recites the final two verses of al-Baqara at night, they suffice him. BAYDAWI-FULL reporting رسول الله صلى الله عليه وسلم: The Prophet reported that two verses from the treasures of Paradise were written by the Merciful’s hand two thousand years before creation; reciting them after the last night prayer suffices in place of night vigil. BAYDAWI-FULL reporting رسول الله صلى الله عليه وسلم: The Prophet said that whoever recites the last two verses of Sūrat al-Baqara at night, they suffice him. NASAFI-FULL: A report says reciting the closing verses beginning «Āmana al-rasūl» at night suffices the reciter. NASAFI-FULL: Another report says reciting those two verses after the final night prayer substitutes for night vigil. IBNKATHIR-FULL reporting أبو مسعود: The Prophet says that whoever recites the two closing verses of al-Baqara at night will find them sufficient; Ibn Kathir cites several transmission routes and gives no grading here. DURR-FULL reporting ابن مسعود: The Prophet said that whoever recites the final two verses of al-Baqara at night will find them sufficient. BURSEVI reporting النبي صلى الله عليه وسلم: A report says two verses from Paradise's treasures were written by the Merciful's hand two thousand years before creation, and reciting them after the final evening prayer suffices for night vigil. BURSEVI reporting النبي صلى الله عليه وسلم: The Prophet says whoever recites the final two verses of al-Baqara will be sufficed, either for night prayer or for the reckoning on Judgment Day. ALUSI-FULL reporting ابن مسعود: The six canonical collections transmit from Ibn Masʿud that whoever recites the final two verses of al-Baqara at night, they suffice him. ALUSI-FULL reporting ابن مسعود الأنصاري: Ibn ʿAdi reports from Ibn Masʿud al-Ansari that two verses from the treasures of Paradise were written by God before creation; reciting them after the night prayer suffices for night worship. ELMALILI reporting İbn Mes‘ûd: A Prophetic report from Ibn Masud, included in the Kutub al-Sitta, says whoever recites the last two verses of al-Baqara at night will find them sufficient. IBNASHUR-FULL reporting أبو مسعود الأنصاري البدري: A report in the Sahih says the Prophet taught that reciting the last two verses of al-Baqara at night suffices the reciter. IBNASHUR-FULL: The sufficiency may mean the two verses replace night prayer, or that they provide blessing and protection from devils and harms; Ibn Ashur suggests both meanings are intended. KURANYOLU-TEFSIR reporting hadis: Many sound hadiths describe the merit of the sūra’s final two verses; one says they suffice at night for whoever recites them, cited from Bukhārī and Shawkānī. STUDYQURAN reporting the Prophet: The Prophet said that reciting the two verses at night suffices the reciter. [21 notes: ABDURRAZZAQ(علقمة بن قيس); KASHSHAF-FULL(النبي صلى الله عليه وسلم); IBNATIYYA-FULL(أبو مسعود عقبة بن عمرو); TABRISI(النبي); QURTUBI-FULL(أبو مسعود الأنصاري); BAYDAWI-FULL(رسول الله صلى الله عليه وسلم); NASAFI-FULL; IBNKATHIR-FULL(أبو مسعود); DURR-FULL(ابن مسعود); BURSEVI(النبي صلى الله عليه وسلم); ALUSI-FULL(ابن مسعود,ابن مسعود الأنصاري); ELMALILI(İbn Mes‘ûd); IBNASHUR-FULL(أبو مسعود الأنصاري البدري); KURANYOLU-TEFSIR(hadis); STUDYQURAN(the Prophet)]
- 2:286/q37/p2 The closing verses came from a treasure beneath the Throne or a precreation book. — ABDURRAZZAQ reporting الحسن: Al-Ḥasan reports that God bestowed the closing verses of Sūrat al-Baqara on the Prophet as treasures from the Throne. THALABI reporting مسروق: Masruq calls Surat al-Baqara and Ali Imran a poor person's treasure when recited late at night. MAWARDI-FULL reporting عقبة بن عامر الجهني: Uqba ibn Amir al-Juhani reports that the Prophet urged recitation of the final two verses of al-Baqara, which God gave him from beneath the Throne. KASHSHAF-FULL reporting علي: Ali says al-Baqara’s closing verses are a treasure beneath the Throne. KASHSHAF-FULL reporting النبي صلى الله عليه وسلم: The Prophet said he was given al-Baqara’s closing verses from a treasure beneath the Throne, a gift no prior prophet received. IBNATIYYA-FULL reporting النبي: The Prophet says he received the final verses from a treasure beneath the Throne, a gift no one before him received. QURTUBI-FULL reporting النبي: The Prophet said these closing verses came from a treasure beneath the Throne and were not given to any prophet before him. NASAFI-FULL reporting على: ʿAlī is cited as saying that the closing verses of al-Baqara are a treasure from beneath the Throne. IBNKATHIR-FULL reporting أبو ذر: Abu Dharr reports the Prophet saying that he received the closing verses of al-Baqara from a treasure beneath the Throne, a gift given to no prophet before him. IBNKATHIR-FULL reporting عقبة بن عامر الجهني: The Prophet tells Uqba ibn Amir to recite the final two verses of al-Baqara, saying they were given from beneath the Throne; Ibn Kathir calls the chain hasan, though the major collections do not include it. IBNKATHIR-FULL reporting حذيفة: Hudhayfa reports the Prophet saying that his community was favored with these verses from a treasure beneath the Throne, given to no one before or after him. IBNKATHIR-FULL reporting علي: Ali says that no person who understands Islam should go to sleep without reciting the closing verses, a treasure granted to the Prophet from beneath the Throne. IBNKATHIR-FULL reporting علي: In another transmission Ali adds Ayat al-Kursi to the nightly recitation of the closing verses, describing both as a treasure beneath the Throne. IBNKATHIR-FULL reporting ابن عباس: Ibn Abbas reports that the Prophet laughed when reciting the closing verses and Ayat al-Kursi, saying they are from the Merciful's treasure beneath the Throne; reciting 4:123 and 53:39–41 instead made him grieve and submit. IBNKATHIR-FULL reporting معقل بن يسار: The Prophet says he was given the Opening of the Book and the closing verses of al-Baqara from beneath the Throne, while the Mufassal was given as an extra voluntary portion. ALUSI-FULL reporting أبو ذر: Al-Hakim, who authenticates it, and al-Bayhaqi report from Abu Dharr that the final verses were granted from beneath the Throne and should be taught to wives and children as prayer, Qur'an, and supplication. ELMALILI reporting Ebû Zer: Abu Dharr’s report, cited by Hakim and Bayhaqi, says the Prophet received the surah’s final two verses from a treasure beneath the Throne and urged people to learn and teach them as prayer, Qur’an, and supplication. STUDYQURAN reporting the Prophet: A report says the Prophet received the two verses sealing the sūrah from a treasury beneath the Throne. [18 notes: ABDURRAZZAQ(الحسن); THALABI(مسروق); MAWARDI-FULL(عقبة بن عامر الجهني); KASHSHAF-FULL(النبي صلى الله عليه وسلم,علي); IBNATIYYA-FULL(النبي); QURTUBI-FULL(النبي); NASAFI-FULL(على); IBNKATHIR-FULL(أبو ذر,ابن عباس,حذيفة,عقبة بن عامر الجهني,علي,معقل بن يسار); ALUSI-FULL(أبو ذر); ELMALILI(Ebû Zer); STUDYQURAN(the Prophet)]
- 2:286/q37/p3 Reciting the closing verses protects a home or reader from Satan. — ABDURRAZZAQ reporting أبو قلابة: Abū Qilāba says that whoever recites the closing verses of al-Baqara keeps Satan out of the home for three days. TAB-FULL: Ṭabarī explains God as the believers’ ally and defender: they believe and obey, while He is faithful to the obedient and opposed to disbelievers; they ask victory as His party against those denying His oneness, worshipping rivals, and obeying Satan. KASHSHAF-FULL: The passage about Moses’ companion says Satan caused him to forget; Satan cannot directly cause forgetting but whispers, leading to the negligence that causes it. QURTUBI-FULL: Another explanation is that the verses suffice against Satan’s harm, so he has no power over the reader. QURTUBI-FULL reporting حذيفة بن اليمان: Al-Dani transmits from Hudhayfa ibn al-Yaman that a book was written before creation and three closing verses from it were revealed; reciting them at home keeps Satan away for three nights. IBNKATHIR-FULL reporting النعمان بن بشير: The Prophet says God wrote a book two thousand years before creating the heavens and earth, sent down from it the two verses ending al-Baqara, and that a house where they are recited is not approached by Satan for three nights. Al-Tirmidhi calls the report gharīb; al-Hakim calls it sahih by Muslim's standard, though they did not include it. DURR-FULL reporting النعمان بن بشير: The Prophet said God wrote a book two thousand years before creating heaven and earth, revealed two of its verses to close al-Baqara, and Satan does not approach a house where they are recited for three nights. BURSEVI reporting النبي صلى الله عليه وسلم: The Prophet urges learning al-Baqara as a blessing, says abandoning it brings regret and sorcerers cannot master it, and the report links a house without its recitation for three nights with Satan's approach. ALUSI-FULL reporting شداد بن أوس: Al-Tabarani, with a good chain, reports from Shaddad ibn Aws that God wrote a book two thousand years before creating heaven and earth, and the final two verses protect a home from Satan for three nights. IBNASHUR-FULL: Choosing the form that can imply compliance for evil suggests Satan commands and the soul obeys, disfavoring sins; using kasaba for good suggests God guides people to it by innate disposition. KURANYOLU-TEFSIR: Although believers might expect humanity to embrace this accessible religion, testing through reason, will, the self, and Satan obstructs many; because the thankful are few, believers need God’s help beyond their own effort. [11 notes: ABDURRAZZAQ(أبو قلابة); TAB-FULL; KASHSHAF-FULL; QURTUBI-FULL(حذيفة بن اليمان); IBNKATHIR-FULL(النعمان بن بشير); DURR-FULL(النعمان بن بشير); BURSEVI(النبي صلى الله عليه وسلم); ALUSI-FULL(شداد بن أوس); IBNASHUR-FULL; KURANYOLU-TEFSIR]
- 2:286/q37/p4 The closing verses were granted during the Night Journey or Ascension. — WAHIDI-BASIT reporting مقاتل بن سليمان: Muqatil reports that during the Night Journey the Prophet received the closing verses of Baqara; angels urged him to ask, Gabriel taught this prayer, and Gabriel answered after each section that God had granted it. BAGHAWI-FULL reporting عبد الله: ʿAbd Allah reports that during the night journey the Prophet reached the lote tree in the sixth heaven, received the five prayers and closing verses of al-Baqara, and was granted forgiveness for members of his community who did not associate partners with God. TABRISI reporting عبد الله بن مسعود: Ibn Masʿūd reports that during the Night Journey the Prophet reached the Lote Tree, received the five prayers and the final verses of al-Baqara, and his community's monotheists were forgiven except for grave sins. RAZI-FULL reporting مقاتل بن سليمان: Al-Wahidi transmits from Muqatil a report that during the night journey the Prophet received the closing verses of al-Baqara and Gabriel taught him these prayers; God answered each petition with forgiveness, no burden, and victory. Some versions add that angels said “Amen”; no grading is given. ABUHAYYAN-FULL reporting الحسن ومجاهد وابن سيرين وابن عباس: One report says these two verses came without Gabriel: the Prophet heard them directly on the Night of Ascension, making them exceptions to the Medinan surah. IBNKATHIR-FULL reporting عبد الله: Ibn Masud reports that during the Night Journey, at the Sidrat al-Muntaha in the sixth heaven, the Prophet received the five prayers, the closing verses of al-Baqara, and forgiveness for non-polytheists from his community even for grave sins. BURSEVI reporting النبي صلى الله عليه وسلم: During the Miʿrāj, the Prophet recited the believers' response to 2:285 and then asked the petitions of this verse; God answered that error, forgetfulness, and coercion were lifted and granted the remaining requests. BURSEVI reporting النبي صلى الله عليه وسلم: In the Miʿrāj report, at the Lote Tree of the Utmost Boundary in the sixth heaven, the Prophet received the five prayers, the closing verses of al-Baqara, and forgiveness for any follower who avoids associating partners with God. STUDYQURAN reporting the Prophet: On the Night Journey and Ascension, the Prophet received the five prayers, the Cow's final two verses, and forgiveness for community members who do not associate partners with God. [9 notes: WAHIDI-BASIT(مقاتل بن سليمان); BAGHAWI-FULL(عبد الله); TABRISI(عبد الله بن مسعود); RAZI-FULL(مقاتل بن سليمان); ABUHAYYAN-FULL(الحسن ومجاهد وابن سيرين وابن عباس); IBNKATHIR-FULL(عبد الله); BURSEVI(النبي صلى الله عليه وسلم); STUDYQURAN(the Prophet)]
- 2:286/q37/p5 The ending and al-Fātiḥa were given as two special lights. — TABRISI reporting ابن عباس: In al-Kalbī's tafsir report from Ibn ʿAbbās, an angel announces two lights not given to a prophet before—the Opening and the end of al-Baqara—and says reciters will receive their request. IBNKATHIR-FULL reporting ابن عباس: Ibn Abbas reports that Gabriel brought news of two lights never given to an earlier prophet: al-Fatiha and the closing verses of al-Baqara; every letter recited from them brings a gift. ITQAN reporting ابن عباس: A report in Muslim says an angel brought the Prophet good news of two lights not given to a prophet before him: al-Fatiha and the final verses of al-Baqara. [3 notes: TABRISI(ابن عباس); IBNKATHIR-FULL(ابن عباس); ITQAN(ابن عباس)]
- 2:286/q37/p6 The Prophet says that the two final verses of Sūrat al-Baqara suffice whoever recites them; no grading is given. — ABDURRAZZAQ reporting النبي صلى الله عليه وسلم: The Prophet says that the two final verses of Sūrat al-Baqara suffice whoever recites them; no grading is given. [1 notes: ABDURRAZZAQ(النبي صلى الله عليه وسلم)]
- 2:286/q37/p7 Al-Suddi says Gabriel instructed Muhammad to recite this petition when the verse was revealed. — TAB-FULL reporting السدي: Al-Suddi says Gabriel instructed Muhammad to recite this petition when the verse was revealed. [1 notes: TAB-FULL(السدي)]
- 2:286/q37/p8 Ibn Abbas reports that as the Prophet recited the end of 2:285 and 2:286, God answered each petition: forgiveness, no accountability, mercy, and victory. — TAB-FULL reporting ابن عباس: Ibn Abbas reports that as the Prophet recited the end of 2:285 and 2:286, God answered each petition: forgiveness, no accountability, mercy, and victory. [1 notes: TAB-FULL(ابن عباس)]
- 2:286/q37/p9 Al-Dahhak says Gabriel instructed the Prophet to recite each successive petition, then confirmed after each that it had been granted. — TAB-FULL reporting الضحاك: Al-Dahhak says Gabriel instructed the Prophet to recite each successive petition, then confirmed after each that it had been granted. [1 notes: TAB-FULL(الضحاك)]
- 2:286/q37/p10 Ibn Abbas reports God answering the Prophet’s recitation: He will not hold the believers accountable or burden them, and has pardoned, forgiven, shown mercy, and granted victory. — TAB-FULL reporting ابن عباس: Ibn Abbas reports God answering the Prophet’s recitation: He will not hold the believers accountable or burden them, and has pardoned, forgiven, shown mercy, and granted victory. [1 notes: TAB-FULL(ابن عباس)]
- 2:286/q37/p11 Wahb ibn Munabbih says reciting both chapters on Friday night brings light between the named cosmic bounds. — THALABI reporting وهب بن منبه: Wahb ibn Munabbih says reciting both chapters on Friday night brings light between the named cosmic bounds. [1 notes: THALABI(وهب بن منبه)]
- 2:286/q37/p12 Masruq says a person who recites Surat al-Baqara in a night will be crowned. — THALABI reporting مسروق: Masruq says a person who recites Surat al-Baqara in a night will be crowned. [1 notes: THALABI(مسروق)]
